boundary points are copied from input.\nvoid stencilIteration(const Real* __restrict input,\n                      Real* __restrict output,\n                      const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t plane = nx * ny;\n    #pragma omp for collapse(2) schedule(static)\n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            const size_t base = idx3(0, y, z, nx, ny);\n            const Real* __restrict in = input + base;\n            Real* __restrict out = output + base;\n            if (z == 0 || z == nz - 1 || y == 0 || y == ny - 1) {\n                // Whole row lies on the boundary\n                std::memcpy(out, in, nx * sizeof(Real));\n                continue;\n            }\n            const Real* __restrict front = in - nx;\n            const Real* __restrict back = in + nx;\n            const Real* __restrict bottom = in - plane;\n            const Real* __restrict top = in + plane;\n            out[0] = in[0];\n            #pragma omp simd\n            for (size_t x = 1; Inside a\n// tile the sweep streams through z, so the three z-planes of the tile stay in\n// cache and each input value is fetched from memory (close to) once. Tiles are\n// distributed statically and identically for initialization and all sweeps, so\n// each thread always works on the memory it touched first.\nstruct Tiling {\n    size_t zb, yb;    // tile extents\n    size_t nzt, nyt; At 512³ it is about 30x faster on this machine (2× EPYC 7763, 256 hardware threads).\n\n**Correctness:** the results hash matched the original exactly for every size and thread count I tried. That covered odd sizes, sizes of 1 and 2 in a dimension, very flat grids, large grids, and 1, 3, 17, 64, 128 and 256 threads. `-v` validation passes. The per-cell sum keeps the original order, and no fast-math flags are used.\n\n**Performance** (million cell updates per second, default settings, no environment variables):\n\n| Grid | Original (1 thread) | OpenMP |\n|---|---|---|\n| 128³ | 493 | ~11,100 |\n| 256³ | 455 | ~21,000 |\n| 512³ | 417 | ~12,200–13,700 |\n\nThe 512³ case is limited by memory bandwidth, not compute.\n\n**Changes in `stencil3d.cpp`:**\n- **One parallel region** wraps the whole iteration loop. The pause between iterations is the automatic wait at the end of each parallel loop, so threads aren't restarted every iteration.\n- **Single pass per iteration:** the inner points and the boundary copy are done in one sweep, row by row, instead of the original second full-grid loop with per-cell boundary checks.\n- **Cache tiling:** work is split into blocks of rows sized to stay in cache, so most values are read from memory only once. This gave about +30% at 512³.\n- **Memory placed near the threads:** the grids are allocated without being zero-filled. The parallel initialization then touches each part of memory on the same thread that later computes it, so memory lands on that thread's own socket. Since `print_results` needs a plain `std::vector`, a copy is made only for `-r`, outside the timed section.\n- **Built-in thread pinning:** this runs only if none of `OMP_PROC_BIND`, `OMP_PLACES`, `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY` or `KMP_AFFINITY` is set. It pins threads to physical cores first and respects `taskset`. Without pinning, threads moved between sockets and speed dropped about 4x (to ~2,100 at 512³).\n- **Cache-bypassing stores:** for grids over 512 MB in total, results are written straight to memory with AVX instructions, gaining about another 20%. When AVX isn't available it falls back to the normal loop.\n- **Validation** loops now use parallel reductions.\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`:** adds `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` and links `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`, so OpenMP is always on. The executable is still `stencil3d`.\n\nTwo things you might trip over:\n- The pinning code reads Linux-specific `/sys` files.
